## The Decoder - Former OpenAI researcher found Nvidia Blackwell fingerprints in six Chinese AI models (2026-11-13)

Former OpenAI researcher says he found Nvidia Blackwell fingerprints hidden inside six Chinese AI models Ad Skip to content The Decoder AI, Menschen, Wirtschaft --> Log In Subscribe DE Switch to German Primary Menu The Decoder AI, Menschen, Wirtschaft --> Log In Subscribe DE Switch to German Primary Menu Sign In Register Subscribe Now The Decoder Opens discord in a new tab Opens LinkedIn in a new tab Plus AI and society Copy the url to clipboard Share this article Go to comment section Former OpenAI researcher says he found Nvidia Blackwell fingerprints hidden inside six Chinese AI models Maximilian Schreiner View the LinkedIn Profile of Maximilian Schreiner Nov 13, 2026 Nano Banana Pro prompted by THE DECODER AI researcher Will DePue says a forensic analysis has turned up the first technical evidence that leading Chinese AI labs have access to Nvidia's latest Blackwell hardware despite US export controls. He says senior researchers at OpenAI and Anthropic have already checked the method. According to a forensic investigation by Will DePue , Chinese AI companies DeepSeek, Moonshot AI, MiniMax, and Ant Group are using Nvidia's export-controlled Blackwell GPUs. DePue played a key role at OpenAI in building the Sora and Sora 2 video models as well as GPT-4o, o3, and GPT-5 before founding the nonprofit research group Unemployment Labs . His analysis, he says, provides the first technical evidence of this access. US officials and news outlets had suspected that DeepSeek and Moonshot AI were using Blackwell chips but never published proof. DePue wrote on X that senior researchers at OpenAI and Anthropic have already independently checked and confirmed his findings. According to DePue, six Chinese models released since April 2026 carry the distinct signature of Nvidia's Blackwell generation, while earlier models such as DeepSeek-V3 and Kimi K2.5 show traces of Nvidia's older Hopper generation. The evidence comes directly from the published language models. An AI model consists of billions of numerical values called weights. Before training starts, PyTorch, the standard software framework, fills these empty slots with random numbers generated from a starting value called a seed. To distribute this massive amount of data quickly, the GPU splits the work across its individual compute cores. Because different chip models have different core counts, the filling process produces a characteristic pattern, according to the report. Older Hopper chips such as the H100 and H800 use 132 compute cores, while the newer Blackwell chips use exactly 148 or 152 cores depending on the model (B200/B300 or GB200/GB300). Training then adjusts these values millions of times, but some slots for unused words or symbols keep their initial random values. Even in heavily trained parts of the model, a faint statistical echo of the initial distribution survives, according to DePue. He says he worked the pattern backward and reproduced the results for DeepSeek-V4-Flash and Kimi K3 exactly on real Blackwell chips. He has published the verification code on GitHub. Training runs costing more than half a billion dollars The analysis maps DeepSeek-V4-Flash and MiniMax-M3 to B200 or B300 chips (148 cores). DeepSeek-V4-Pro, Kimi K3, Kimi K3.1-Pro, and Ant Group's Ling-3.1-flash show, according to the analysis, the 152-core pattern of Nvidia's liquid-cooled GB200 or GB300 flagship rack systems. DePue says chance can be ruled out statistically and that the method correctly identified all 24 control models with known hardware, including Meta's Llama 3.1 (H100). Image: Will DePue / Unemployment Labs DePue's report estimates that the five training runs he examined required about 12,000 Blackwell GPUs combined, as much computing power as roughly 30,000 older H100 chips. Kimi K3 alone required an estimated 4,500 Blackwell processors over about 70 days, and DeepSeek-V4-Pro about 3,000. At a unit price of about $70,000, each of those clusters would cost $200 million to $300 million, and all five systems together more than half a billion dollars. The findings also align with analysis from Dylan Patel, chief analyst at SemiAnalysis. In an interview with Sequoia Capital, Patel said DeepSeek-V3's model architecture was still specifically tailored for Nvidia's Hopper chips, while DeepSeek-V4's architecture was built for Blackwell GPUs and Huawei's Ascend chips. According to DePue's report, models from other Chinese labs, including Alibaba's Qwen3.8 and those from Zhipu, Tencent, Xiaomi, and StepFun, still show only Hopper signatures, while Meituan's LongCat-2.0 points to H


## The Decoder - Anthropic IPO six times oversubscribed, on track to be largest offering in history (2026-11-13)

Anthropic's IPO is six times oversubscribed and on track to become the largest public offering in history Ad Skip to content The Decoder AI, Menschen, Wirtschaft --> Log In Subscribe DE Switch to German Primary Menu The Decoder AI, Menschen, Wirtschaft --> Log In Subscribe DE Switch to German Primary Menu Sign In Register Subscribe Now The Decoder Opens discord in a new tab Opens LinkedIn in a new tab Short News Copy the url to clipboard Share this article Go to comment section Anthropic's IPO is six times oversubscribed and on track to become the largest public offering in history Maximilian Schreiner View the LinkedIn Profile of Maximilian Schreiner Nov 13, 2026 Anthropic is on track for the largest IPO ever, with orders exceeding available shares by a factor of six. That's according to Bloomberg . The Claude developer and its co-founders aim to raise up to $64.4 billion through the public offering and another $26 billion via a private placement, which would surpass even SpaceX's IPO. The target valuation sits at roughly $1.4 trillion, with existing investors getting priority in share allocation. The company's financials likely fuel much of the demand. Anthropic's annualized revenue climbed to $81 billion by late October, up from $71 billion in September. Nvidia, whose investors have grown more cautious since CEO Jensen Huang was ousted over an alleged espionage scandal, is itself buying $10 billion in Anthropic stock through the private placement. Goldman Sachs and Morgan Stanley are managing the Nasdaq listing under the ticker ANT, and CEO Dario Amodei will pitch investors alongside JPMorgan CEO Jamie Dimon in a Friday livestream.
